# TOPAK — Open-Findings Register

**2026.11.11** · derived · a register, not a record · basis: the tree at commit `bbda621` (Genesis Checkpoint G11,
`9d6f95f444455510`; Theory of Operation revision 15, `e737aa042625678d`; sixty-seven record files; `spec/`, `floor/`,
`policies/`, `mechanism/`), and every dated document under `derived/` · one of five documents of the self review of
2026.11.11, whose head is `topak-critical-review-2026.11.11.md` · Parts I and II were compiled by five graders, Part III by
thirteen readers, the lead and fourteen verifiers, each a fresh context of the same model lineage that drafted the corpus · nothing
was changed in the tree · prepared by the assistant at the custodian's request

Nothing here is ratified, and this document proposes no decision text. It mints no identifier for an earlier
finding: every row of Parts I and II keeps the identifier its source gave it. Part III's identifiers are the
readers' (`K1-3`, `I2-12`); the review gives the most serious of them `CR-` tags, continuing the series.

**Not independent.** Every grader, reader and verifier is the same kind of model as the drafter of the corpus, and
of this register. A grader read the earlier findings with the tree; a reader read the tree without them; a
verifier checked each finding against the files and did not find it. That is independence of context, and not of
lineage, and it is not the reader ADR-0029 A2 asks for. The A2.5 line stays "None / None".

---

## 0. What this is

The corpus has reviewed itself in dated documents under `derived/`: three critical reviews, two inventories, a
design-gap assessment, two external assessments, a feature list with its own lists of exposures and stale
sentences, three readings of the command-chain proposal, and a handful of notes that each end "suggested entries,
none made". Each is frozen, and each was written against the state of record of its day. Most of what they found
was never dispositioned, and no document says how any of it stands today.

This register is that document. It has three parts.

- **Part I** (460 rows) gathers every finding, gap, exposure, stale sentence and suggested entry of those
  documents, with each record's Open obligations, the fifteen pending amendments, and what has moved in G11's open
  list and the reference's own register. Each row is graded **at the pin**: opened in the tree at `bbda621` and
  checked there.
- **Part II** (47 rows) takes the derived documents themselves, one row each: what each stands on, what has moved
  under it, whether the index says so, and whether any manifest measures it.
- **Part III** (493 findings) is new: what this review's thirteen cold readers and its lead found, each
  checked by a verifier that did not find it. 485 stand and 8 were dropped.

It is the table to disposition from. A finding is applied as a change, declined with reasons, or carried as named
debt (ADR-0029 A2.1); this register does none of the three.

## 1. How to read it

**Status at the pin**, Parts I and II:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **open** | Still true of the tree, and confirmed there |
| **partly addressed** | The tree holds a change that answers part of it; the rest stands |
| **appears addressed** | The tree holds a change that answers it. Not "closed": nobody but the drafter has said so, and many of these answers are records ratified by their drafter or text that is only Proposed |
| **superseded** | What it was about is gone or replaced |
| **cannot tell** | The tree cannot decide it: an external source absent from the tree, or a one-off analysis with no successor |

**Carried by** names where the corpus itself now tracks an item: an item of G11's open list, a record's Open
obligations, a row of `mechanism/GAPS-AND-ASSUMPTIONS.md`, a later derived document, or **nowhere**.

**The annex has the rest.** Each row here gives the statement, what it is against, its status and who carries it.
The line of the source, the kind, the basis, and the `file:line` each grader opened to grade the row are in
`topak-self-review-annex-2026.11.11/register.tsv`. Part III's findings are in full, with each verifier's verdict and
correction, in `findings/` beside it, one file per reader.

**Counts.**

| | Rows | open | partly addressed | appears addressed | superseded | cannot tell |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Part I: earlier findings | 460 | 252 | 101 | 80 | 10 | 17 |
| Part II: derived documents | 47 | 13 | | | 12 | 22 |

Part I by kind: 102 open-obligation rows, 91 suggested entry, 77 findings, 72 stale sentences, 45 gaps, 39 exposures, 18 pending amendments, 16 questions. **108 of the 252 open rows are carried by no list in the corpus** (§5).

Part III: 493 reported, 348 confirmed as written, 137 confirmed with a correction,
8 dropped. Standing, by severity: High 1 · Medium-High 11 · Medium 96 · Low-Medium 191 · Low 186.

---

## 2. Part I: earlier findings, at the pin

By source, in the order the sources were written. A source's own identifiers are kept. Where a later
source restates an earlier one's item the two rows both stand; §5 lists the repeats.

## 3. Part II: the derived documents, as documents

One row for the index and one for each document. `open` means the document is the current one of its kind
and is behind the tree; `superseded` means the index or a later document says so; `cannot tell` is a one-off
analysis with no successor, where the question of currency does not arise. The review's five documents supersede
four of these as basis or as current: the 10.02 critical review, the 11.05 feature list, the 10.09 residual-risk
statement and the 09.28 prioritization.

## 4. Part III: this review's findings, as verified

Thirteen readers each read one part of the tree cold, on a copy from which `derived/` (but for the 11.10b
proposal) and `diagrams/README.md` were removed, so that none had seen an earlier finding. Each returned findings
in the Reviewer's Guide's form: what it is against, the claim, why it is wrong, where the fix belongs. The lead
added eleven from running the corpus's own checkers. Every finding then went to a verifier that had not found it,
with the text at the lines it cites, to be confirmed, corrected or dropped.

A row below gives the finding's one-line statement. **Severity** is the verifier's where it differs from the
reader's. "Already on the corpus's own lists", the claim, the conflicting text and the verifier's correction are
in the annex. Severity scale: High (a guarantee, a ratification or a safety claim is wrong or unsupported),
Medium-High, Medium (two layers disagree on substance), Low-Medium, Low (a stale sentence or count with no
consequence for a claim).

### 4.1 Reader A: ADR-0001 to ADR-0023 (41 reported; 30 confirmed, 11 confirmed with a correction, 0 dropped)

Standing, by severity: Medium-High 1 · Medium 9 · Low-Medium 11 · Low 20.

### 4.15 Dropped at verification (8)

Listed so that a later reader does not report them again without the verifier's reason.

| Id | Reader's statement | Why it was dropped |
|---|---|---|
| C-23 | ADR-0054 calls the third grant path "ADR-0031 A3"; in ADR-0031 it is A4, and A3 is another amendment, in force | The texts do not conflict. Read in context, each item of ADR-0054's debt list names the existing clause the debt is against, with the change owed in parentheses, and :301 names the amendment drafted to pay it: 'ADR-0024 A3 (a workflow is a set of seatings and a graph)' is paid by ADR-0024 A4, 'ADR-0037 §4 (a sixth seat kind)' by ADR-0037 A5, and 'ADR-0031 A3 (a third grant path)' by ADR-0031 A4. ADR-0031 A3 is the in-force 'who grants' text (two paths) that a third path is added beside; ADR-0054.md:105 cites it in that sense, and :305 asks that ADR-0024 A3, the existing 'A promotion seats', be ratified first. ADR-0054 does not call the third grant path A3. |
| C-48 | §5 says "Run has no tally" and, one paragraph on, names one | The texts do not conflict. 'The earlier paragraph was not qualified' is not so: it reads 'enforced only through the declaration until something in Run can stall on it', and R8 opens 'A workflow's promotion is such a place', taking up that qualifier. R8's rule rests on ADR-0054, which is Proposed, and says of itself 'Until this record is ratified the rule is declared and not enforced', so Consequences' 'Run is enforced only by declaration' is still accurate. |
| C-52 | ADR-0036 finding 1 quotes ADR-0035's ingress-bounded round in words the record no longer has | The finding already attributes the sentence to the superseded revision: it is headed 'ADR-0035 R1 assumes tools run inside the guest' and 'Its ingress-bounded round says' refers to R1. The record also carries the disposition: its Open obligations strike 'ADR-0035 finding 1's disposition' as 'Disposed by ADR-0035 R4 (2026.09.30)'. Nothing presents the quotation as ADR-0035's current text, and the fix asked for (mark it as R1's) is what the finding's first words do. R1's wording is not in this tree, so the quotation cannot be checked either way. |
| F-42 | ADR-0050's provenance note counts eleven operator requests; its Context table and its own arithmetic give ten | The texts do not conflict. The note counts the requests in `ops.rs`; the Context table lists the ten that have an effect on authority (its second column) and nowhere says it is the whole list. The eleventh is `status`, a read: mechanism/README.md:67-71 lists `grant`, `renew`, `end_round`, `revoke`, `fence_epoch`, `issue`, `gates_passed`, `register_profile`, `launch` and `status`, with `break_glass_approve` retired, and names the five later additions apart. Line 60's 'Five requests add authority' counts adds, not requests. At most the record could name the eleventh. |
| I1-8 | The `federation` stage has no entry for the verifier's whole-chain check; FD2 names only the granting plane's charter, which is the check `M_LASTLINK` shows insufficient | The texts do not conflict. The absence in ADR-0067 is real, but no text says the floor carries the whole-chain or the fence check, and the corpus does say where they live. ADR-0059 §1 (:66-67) gives the field-by-field comparison to "every verifier of the chain (ADR-0033 §4)", which is the mechanism check that ADR-0033.md:124-128 keeps apart from the floor ("The floor and bundle are not re-run at apply"); §6 (:121-122) gives the epoch comparison to "an admit or a permit"; and §9 puts "a lease refused by a verifier that checks the whole chain" on the reference item (:161-162), beside and apart from the floor item (:160). FD2 forbids a grant, the granting plane's own act; M_LASTLINK mutates the verifier's acceptance (ChainChecked, spec/Federation.tla:108-110), so FD2 is not "the mutated check". The finding itself allows this reading ("It may be intended that the chain walk is mechanism and not floor"). |
| I1-16 | The FINDING blocks in five module headers, and one rows-file comment, quote or describe record text that R1 has since replaced | The headers do describe text that R1 replaced, but the corpus reconciles it where step 3 of the check looks. Each record's R1 History row names "the module's header" as its source and says how the finding was taken (ADR-0059.md:245, ADR-0061.md:204, ADR-0063.md:202, ADR-0065.md:194, ADR-0066.md:177), and spec/README.md:158 already carries the line the finding asks for: "Each module's header states what it found against its record, and the R1 row of each of ADR-0059 to ADR-0066 (2026.11.10) cites its modules and results files by digest and says how the record took the findings". Each module's legend reads `Mut = "none": ADR-00NN as drafted`. Two supporting statements are also wrong: "cannot remove any" is in decisions/ADR-0065.md (:194), and the quotations do match text in the corpus (the proposal at derived/topak-federated-c2-design-proposal-2026.11.10b.md:194 and :892, and ADR-0059.md:245); only the quotation at Release.tla:45 is found nowhere else. |
| J-28 | The register and the checkpoints date the last live smoke run to 2026.09.25; ADR-0033 says it was not rerun that day | The texts do not conflict. The same Evidence row dates the live smoke run to commit `4fd9677` (2026.09.24) and gives `453801c` (2026.09.25) 44 tests and no live run, and G3 says the same of the two runs ("the live smoke run not repeated" on 09.25). "Not repeated since 2026.09.25" is true of the interval that begins that day and agrees with ADR-0033's "Not rerun" in the 09.25 addendum and "as at 2026.09.25" in the next. No text places a live run on 09.25 or on `453801c`; at most the phrase is loose. |
| K2-34 | The reviewer's guide says three proof modules lack machine-checked proofs; G11 says every obligation is proved and three modules do not certify | The texts do not conflict. The guide's own §8 (REVIEWERS:234) defines *certified* as 'every obligation's proof was machine-checked or is accounted for', and the checkpoints use the same gloss (G10:395, 'Twenty-two modules' invariants are proved, nineteen with checked proofs'). So :47, 'Twenty-one of twenty-four proof modules have machine-checked proofs; three do not', is G11:163's 'Twenty-one certify' in the corpus's own words and does not deny that all 5,416 obligations are proved. In the corpus the prover's backends are trusted (T-0045-31) and it is the certified run that checks their proofs (spec/README.md:77-80), so 'proved by the prover is a machine check' is not its usage. What is left is a wording preference (the row could say *certify*) and the 24 against the tree's 32, which K2-1 and K2-25 already carry. |

## 5. What the register shows

Twelve things the gathering shows, each from the graders' notes and each checkable in the annex.

1. **108 open items are carried by no list.** They are true of the tree, a dated document says so, and
   neither G11's open list, nor a record's obligations, nor the reference's register holds them. The table below
   says where they are.
2. **None of the format inventory's reference-code findings is fixed, and almost none is tracked** (its findings
   11 to 14 and 19 to 23: unpinned OPA and Kafka images, a values digest recorded nowhere, non-canonical evidence
   files under canonical digests, a smoke script that reads a key the grant reply no longer has, one bad bundle
   file stopping the node). The design rows of the same week mostly moved, as Batches 27 to 34 answered or
   converted them; the reference rows did not.
3. **A stale sentence is fixed only when its file is rewritten at a cut.** All twenty-two of the 11.05 feature
   list's are still there, and 59 of the 72 stale-sentence rows are open. "Not reported upstream" alone stands in
   the Theory of Operation twice, the glossary twice, ADR-0046 once and the 10.09 residual-risk statement once,
   beside G11 item 104, which is frozen, against a ratified amendment that says it was reported on 2026.10.18.
4. **"Appears addressed" mostly means a record now exists, ratified by its drafter.** CR-32 is answered by
   ADR-0047 and ADR-0048, which are confirmation-required, and CR-29 by ADR-0052, which is provisional. Where the
   answer exists only as Proposed text (CR-3, CR-15) the row is `partly addressed`.
5. **Open obligations move only when a dependent entry touches them.** Twenty-eight bullets across the records are
   met and unstruck ("the ratification run", "the dependent entries", "the narrative owes X"). In the other
   direction, four Proposed records' dependent entries are undrafted: ADR-0051, ADR-0055, ADR-0056 and ADR-0058.
6. **The reference's register is appended to, not rewritten.** Early rows keep "pending A7", "Proposed" and "not
   built" beside later rows that say the opposite, in a file whose header says it was brought current.
7. **The diagram findings of 2026.11.10 are carried by no record.** The second revision of the overlay records
   (R2, the same day) postdates them and answers none, though it edited two of the sections they name.
8. **Seventeen suggested entries from four notes are untouched** (the mathematics tranche, the quorum lattice, the
   use cases, the spec-driven alternatives): fifteen open, two partly addressed.
9. **The same item under several identifiers.** No independent reviewer: CR-5, CR-21, CR-28 and the 10.02
   review's first ask. The image that passes the confinement profile: CR-1, CR-27 and its second ask.
   Placeholders: CR-36 and its seventh. Binding-table rows: seven records and G11 item 90. A disposition of one
   should name the others.
10. **`narrative/architecture-description.md` reuses the tags CR-1 to CR-12** for correspondence rules, so a search
    for a critical-review tag returns both.
11. **Fourteen of forty-six derived documents are measured by no manifest**, among them the three that the living
    documents treat as current (the 10.09 alignment, the 10.09 residual-risk statement, the 11.05 feature list).
    Seven files the index calls "never revised" carry later content commits. Twelve have no title, date or basis
    line. `topak-assessment-2026.10.03.md` has never had an index row, though G10 and G11 measure it.
12. **Five rows that could not be graded** are against a draft article that is not in the tree, and eleven against
    external sources absent from it.

## 6. What was verified, and how

- **Parts I and II.** Five graders, each with read-only access to the tree at `bbda621` and a fixed brief, took
  the sources between them. Every row carries a `file:line` the grader opened at the pin (the annex's `evidence`
  column). A grader did not take a later document's word where the tree could be read. No grader's row was checked
  by a second grader; the lead read each grader's notes and a sample of rows.
- **Part III.** Thirteen readers on a copy without the earlier findings, one fixed brief; fourteen verifiers, one
  fixed brief, each given the findings of a reader it was not (the fourteenth had the lead's), with the text at
  every cited line. The briefs are in the annex (`method/`).
- **What the readers were not shown is how their reading was scored.** The review (§6) reports how many of the
  open, concrete items of Part I the readers found again without having seen them.

## What this does not claim

- That an `appears addressed` row is closed. The custodian has dispositioned none of these.
- That a finding of Part III is right because a verifier confirmed it. The verifier is of the drafter's lineage.
- That what no row names is sound. A finding not made is not a finding of soundness, and the review's ledger says
  which rows of the tree were read against something and which were only read.
- That any status here is the state of record. G11 is.

  (Part III in full, with verdicts), `ledger.tsv`, `method/` and `runs/`.
